================================================================================
FILE: .github/workflows/rpm.yml
================================================================================
name: Build RPM

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
    tags: ["v*"]
  pull_request:
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: write   # needed to attach the RPM to a release

jobs:
  rpm:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    container: fedora:latest
    steps:
      - name: Install build tools
        run: dnf install -y rpm-build python3-devel desktop-file-utils make tar gzip sed grep

      - uses: actions/checkout@v4

      - name: Build RPM
        run: make rpm

      - uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: collective-scanner-rpm
          path: dist/*.rpm

      - name: Publish release
        if: startsWith(github.ref, 'refs/tags/v')
        uses: softprops/action-gh-release@v2
        with:
          files: dist/*.rpm
          generate_release_notes: true

================================================================================
FILE: DESCRIPTION.txt
================================================================================
Collective Scanner is a free, private Linux dashboard showing CPU, memory, disks, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, home network devices, open ports and live per-app traffic in one modern page with pastel light and dark themes. Export to CSV or PDF. Runs locally, needs no root, no tracking. GPLv3.

FILE: Makefile

# Build helpers for Collective Scanner.
#   make run      start the app from this folder
#   make tarball  create the source archive in dist/
#   make rpm      build the RPM package into dist/   (needs: sudo dnf install rpm-build python3-devel desktop-file-utils)
#   make source-txt  write the full source code into one text file in dist/
#   make clean    remove build output

NAME    := collective-scanner
VERSION := $(shell sed -n 's/^__version__ = "\(.*\)"/\1/p' collective.py)
TOP     := $(CURDIR)/build/rpmbuild
FILES   := collective.py traffic.py sysmon.py diskusage.py wifiscan.py bluetoothscan.py lanscan.py \
           data LICENSE README.md PRIVACY.md $(NAME).spec

SRC_TXT := dist/$(NAME)-$(VERSION)-source.txt

.PHONY: run tarball rpm source-txt clean

run:
	python3 collective.py --open

tarball:
	@mkdir -p dist
	tar --create --gzip --file dist/$(NAME)-$(VERSION).tar.gz \
	    --transform 's,^,$(NAME)-$(VERSION)/,' --exclude=__pycache__ $(FILES)
	@echo "Created dist/$(NAME)-$(VERSION).tar.gz"

rpm: tarball
	@grep -q '^Version: *$(VERSION)$$' $(NAME).spec || { echo "Version in $(NAME).spec does not match collective.py ($(VERSION))"; exit 1; }
	@mkdir -p $(TOP)/SOURCES
	cp dist/$(NAME)-$(VERSION).tar.gz $(TOP)/SOURCES/
	rpmbuild -ba --define "_topdir $(TOP)" $(NAME).spec
	cp $(TOP)/RPMS/noarch/$(NAME)-$(VERSION)-*.rpm $(TOP)/SRPMS/$(NAME)-$(VERSION)-*.src.rpm dist/
	@echo; echo "Done. Packages are in dist/:"; ls -1 dist/*.rpm

source-txt:
	@mkdir -p dist
	@{ echo "Collective Scanner $(VERSION) - complete source code"; \
	   echo "Copyright (C) 2026 Denis - licensed under GPL-3.0-or-later"; \
	   echo "https://github.com/agent-denis/collective-scanner"; \
	   echo; echo "Files:"; git ls-files | sed 's/^/  /'; \
	   for f in $$(git ls-files); do \
	     echo; echo "================================================================================"; \
	     echo "FILE: $$f"; \
	     echo "================================================================================"; \
	     cat "$$f"; \
	   done; } > $(SRC_TXT)
	@echo "Created $(SRC_TXT) ($$(wc -l < $(SRC_TXT)) lines)"

clean:
	rm -rf build dist __pycache__

================================================================================
FILE: PRIVACY.md
================================================================================
# Privacy Policy — Collective Scanner

*Last updated: 16 November 2026 · applies to Collective Scanner version 1.0.0 and later*

Collective Scanner is a free, open-source program (GNU GPL v3.0 or later) that runs entirely on your own computer. This policy explains what information it handles and what it does with it.

## Summary

- **No data collection.** Collective Scanner does not collect, store, sell or share any personal data. The author receives nothing.
- **No accounts, analytics, advertising, telemetry or crash reporting.**
- **Everything stays on your computer.** The dashboard is served only on `127.0.0.1` (localhost), so other devices cannot open it.
- **No third-party resources.** The page loads no external fonts, scripts, images or trackers.

## What information the program reads

To show its dashboard, the program reads information **locally** from your computer, while it is running:

| Section | Information read | Source |
|---|---|---|
| System | Processor, memory, storage, temperature and battery levels; names, owners and resource use of running processes | `/proc`, `/sys` |
| Disk | Drive sizes; names and sizes of files and folders you choose to explore | the file system |
| Wi-Fi | Names (SSIDs), hardware addresses, signal strength and security type of nearby wireless networks | NetworkManager (`nmcli`) |
| Bluetooth | Names, addresses, type and signal strength of nearby Bluetooth devices | BlueZ (`bluetoothctl`, D-Bus) |
| Network | Your IP address, router, DNS servers, link speed; addresses, names and makers of devices on your home network | `ip`, `nmcli`, `ping`, `avahi-resolve` |
| Traffic | Network connections of **your own user's** programs: program name, remote address and port, and the number of bytes sent and received | `ss`, `/proc/net/dev` |

**The content of your network traffic is never read, recorded or inspected.** Only connection details and byte counts are used.

This information is held **in memory only** while the program runs and is gone when it stops. Nothing is written to disk, except files you choose to export (see below).

## Network activity performed by the program

Collective Scanner does not contact the author or any server run by the author. It does make the following network requests in order to work:

1. **Connectivity check** (Network section and Overview, about once a minute while they are open): one ping to your router, one ping to `1.1.1.1` (Cloudflare's public DNS service), and one DNS lookup of `fedoraproject.org`. These reveal your public IP address to those services, as any normal internet use does.
2. **Reverse-DNS lookups** (Traffic section): the addresses your programs connect to are looked up through **your computer's configured DNS server**, so that names such as "google.com" can be shown instead of numbers.
3. **Wi-Fi and Bluetooth scans**: your adapters listen for, and may briefly announce themselves to, nearby devices. This is the same as opening your system's Wi-Fi or Bluetooth settings. The program never connects or pairs.
4. **Home-network scan** (only when you press "Find devices"): sends one ping to each address on your local network.
5. **Port check** (only when you press "Start check"): connects to the ports of the device you enter, and may read the short greeting a service sends back.

Only scan networks and devices that you own or have permission to test.

## Information stored on your computer

- **Browser storage:** the page remembers your theme choice (light or dark) and the last section you opened, in your browser's local storage. This never leaves your browser. You can clear it with your browser's "clear site data" option.
- **Exports:** CSV and PDF files are created **only when you click Export**, and are saved where you choose (normally your Downloads folder). They may contain the information listed above, such as addresses, device names and program names. Treat them as private and take care when sharing them.

## Children

The program collects no data from anyone, including children.

## Changes to this policy

Any change will be published in this file in the project's repository at <https://github.com/agent-denis/collective-scanner>, with a new "Last updated" date.

## Contact

Questions about this policy: **denis.solihull@pm.me**, or open an issue at <https://github.com/agent-denis/collective-scanner/issues>.

================================================================================
FILE: README.md
================================================================================
# Collective Scanner

**A free, private dashboard for your Linux computer.** It shows your system, disks, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, home network, open ports and the live network traffic of your own programs, all on one modern page with soft pastel light and dark themes.

- Runs entirely on your computer. Nothing is sent to the author or anyone else.
- No root or sudo needed, and nothing extra to install on Fedora.
- Export any page as **CSV** (Excel / LibreOffice) or as a **PDF report**.
- Free software under the **GNU GPL v3.0 or later**.

---

## Features

| Section | What it shows |
|---|---|
| **Overview** | Key numbers from every section at a glance: processor, memory, drive, download/upload speed, connections, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, network health, temperature |
| **System** | Processor load per core, memory and swap, storage and disk activity, temperatures, battery, top processes by CPU or memory, 60-second history chart |
| **Disk** | Every drive with used and free space, plus a folder explorer that shows what takes up the space |
| **Wi-Fi** | Wireless networks nearby: signal strength, bands, security (open networks are highlighted) and every access point |
| **Bluetooth** | Bluetooth devices nearby, their type, maker and how close they are. It only listens, and never pairs or connects |
| **Network** | Your wired connection, router / internet / DNS health, and the devices on your home network (with maker names) |
| **Ports** | Check which services a device has open, with a plain-language verdict for each open port |
| **Traffic** | Live incoming and outgoing traffic of *your* programs: which program talks to which website, how much data, current speed, where it goes (internet or home network), and which ports your programs are listening on |

**Also included:**
- **Light / dark theme switch.** It follows your system setting at first and remembers your choice.
- **Keyboard shortcuts.** Press **1–8** to jump between sections.
- **Works on a phone-sized screen too.**
- **App-menu entry.** Start it like any other app, and right-click the icon to stop it.

## Install (Fedora)

1. Download the latest `.noarch.rpm` from the [Releases](https://github.com/agent-denis/collective-scanner/releases) page.
2. Install it:

   ```sh
   sudo dnf install ./collective-scanner-1.0.0-1.*.noarch.rpm
   ```

3. Start **Collective Scanner** from the app menu, or in a terminal:

   ```sh
   collective-scanner --open
   ```

The dashboard opens in your browser at <http://127.0.0.1:8780>.

### Stop it

- **Started from the app menu:** right-click the Collective Scanner icon and choose **Stop Collective Scanner**.
- **Started in a terminal:** press **Ctrl+C**.

### Update to a new version

```sh
sudo dnf install ./collective-scanner-<new-version>.noarch.rpm
```

### Uninstall

```sh
sudo dnf remove collective-scanner
```

## Using it

### Command-line options

| Option | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `--open` | open the dashboard in your web browser |
| `--port 9000` | use another port (default `8780`) |
| `--host 127.0.0.1` | address to listen on (default: this computer only) |
| `--version` | show the version |

### Export

Click **Export** in the top-right corner. It always exports the page you are on.

- **CSV file.** Saved to your Downloads folder, for example `collective-scanner_traffic_2026-11-16_0633.csv`. Each page's file holds a few separate tables, and numbers stay as plain numbers, so you can sort and add them up.
- **PDF report.** Opens the print window. Choose **Save as PDF** as the printer. The report always uses the light theme, has a heading with the date and computer name, and leaves out buttons and menus.

Disk, Ports and the Network devices list export what you have scanned, so run the scan first.

## Privacy and safety

- The dashboard only listens on `127.0.0.1`, so only your own computer can open it.
- The traffic scanner reads connection details with `ss`. It **never reads the content** of your traffic.
- No data is collected or sent to the author or anyone else: no accounts, no analytics, no tracking.
- The page uses your computer's own fonts and makes no requests to third-party websites.
- Network activity is limited to the checks the dashboard performs:
  - a connectivity check (ping to 1.1.1.1 and a DNS lookup of fedoraproject.org)
  - reverse-DNS lookups to show website names
  - Wi-Fi and Bluetooth scans
  - the home-network and port scans you start yourself
- **Only scan networks and devices you own or have permission to test.**

Full details are in the [Privacy Policy](PRIVACY.md).

## Troubleshooting

| Problem | Fix |
|---|---|
| `Port 8780 is already in use` | It is already running. Open <http://127.0.0.1:8780>, stop the other copy with `pkill -f collective.py`, or use `--port 8781` |
| Wi-Fi shows "switched off" or 0 networks | Turn Wi-Fi on in Settings → Wi-Fi (scanning works even when not connected) |
| Bluetooth shows "switched off" | Turn Bluetooth on in Settings → Bluetooth |
| Network says "no wired network port found" | Your computer has no Ethernet port, or uses Wi-Fi only. Traffic still works |
| Some folders show "unreadable" in Disk | They belong to the system. Run with `sudo` if you need to include them |
| Traffic shows "–" for some amounts | Data amounts are measured for TCP connections only. UDP connections are listed without amounts |

## Requirements

- Linux with Python 3.9 or newer (developed and tested on Fedora 45).
- Standard tools, all installed by default on Fedora Workstation:
  - `ss`, `ip` (iproute)
  - `ping` (iputils)
  - `nmcli` (NetworkManager)
  - `bluetoothctl` (BlueZ)
  - optional: `avahi-resolve`, for device names
- Python standard library only. There are no pip packages.

## Run from source

```sh
git clone https://github.com/agent-denis/collective-scanner.git
cd collective-scanner
python3 collective.py --open
```

Each scanner also runs on its own as a small web page, for example `python3 wifiscan.py`.

## Build the RPM yourself

```sh
sudo dnf install rpm-build python3-devel desktop-file-utils make
make rpm          # packages appear in dist/
make source-txt   # full source code as one text file in dist/
```

**Making a new release:**

1. Change `__version__` in `collective.py`.
2. Change `Version:` in `collective-scanner.spec`, and add a `%changelog` entry.
3. Commit, then tag it: `git tag v1.0.1 && git push --tags`.

GitHub Actions then builds the RPM and attaches it to a new release automatically.

## Project layout

```
collective.py             web server and the whole dashboard (HTML/CSS/JS inside)
traffic.py                live traffic of the current user's programs
sysmon.py                 system monitor (CPU, memory, temperatures, processes)
diskusage.py              drives and folder sizes
wifiscan.py               Wi-Fi scanner (nmcli)
bluetoothscan.py          Bluetooth scanner (BlueZ)
lanscan.py                wired network, home-network devices, port checker
data/                     launcher, app-menu entry, icon
collective-scanner.spec   RPM recipe
Makefile                  build commands (rpm, tarball, source-txt)
.github/workflows/        automatic RPM build on GitHub
PRIVACY.md                privacy policy
DESCRIPTION.txt           short project description
LICENSE                   GNU GPL v3.0
```

FILE: bluetoothscan.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Copyright (C) 2026 Denis
"""Bluetooth Nearby - a tiny local web app that shows the Bluetooth devices around you.

Run:   python3 bluetoothscan.py        then open http://127.0.0.1:8767
Opts:  --port 9000   --host 127.0.0.1   --scan-seconds 8

Uses BlueZ (`bluetoothctl` + D-Bus via `busctl`), installed on Fedora by default.
Python standard library only. It only *looks* for devices - it never pairs or connects.
"""

import argparse
import json
import subprocess
import threading
import time
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse

SCAN_SECONDS = 8
_scan_lock = threading.Lock()
_last_seen = {}  # address -> unix time we last got a signal reading

# A few well-known Bluetooth company IDs (from manufacturer data).
COMPANIES = {
    0x004C: "Apple", 0x0006: "Microsoft", 0x0075: "Samsung", 0x00E0: "Google",
    0x0087: "Garmin", 0x0059: "Nordic Semiconductor", 0x012D: "Sony",
    0x0171: "Amazon", 0x009E: "Bose", 0x0157: "Huami (Amazfit)",
    0x038F: "Xiaomi", 0x02E5: "Espressif", 0x000F: "Broadcom", 0x000A: "Qualcomm",
    0x001D: "Qualcomm", 0x0002: "Intel", 0x0078: "Nike", 0x00D7: "Qualcomm",
    0x0310: "SGL Italia", 0x02FF: "Silicon Labs",
}

# BlueZ "Icon" values -> (emoji, friendly kind)
ICONS = {
    "audio-headset": ("🎧", "Headset"), "audio-headphones": ("🎧", "Headphones"),
    "audio-card": ("🔊", "Speaker / audio"), "phone": ("📱", "Phone"),
    "computer": ("💻", "Computer"), "input-keyboard": ("⌨️", "Keyboard"),
    "input-mouse": ("🖱️", "Mouse"), "input-gaming": ("🎮", "Game controller"),
    "input-tablet": ("✍️", "Tablet / pen"), "video-display": ("📺", "TV / display"),
    "camera-photo": ("📷", "Camera"), "camera-video": ("🎥", "Video camera"),
    "printer": ("🖨️", "Printer"), "modem": ("📡", "Modem"),
    "network-wireless": ("📶", "Network device"), "scanner": ("🖨️", "Scanner"),
    "multimedia-player": ("🎵", "Media player"),
}

# Bluetooth LE "Appearance" category (value >> 6) -> (emoji, kind)
APPEARANCE = {
    1: ("📱", "Phone"), 2: ("💻", "Computer"), 3: ("⌚", "Watch"), 4: ("⏰", "Clock"),
    5: ("📺", "Display"), 6: ("🎛️", "Remote control"), 7: ("👓", "Glasses"),
    8: ("🏷️", "Tracker tag"), 9: ("🔑", "Key ring"), 10: ("🎵", "Media player"),
    12: ("🌡️", "Thermometer"), 13: ("❤️", "Heart-rate sensor"),
    14: ("🩺", "Blood pressure monitor"), 15: ("⌨️", "Input device"),
    16: ("🩸", "Glucose meter"), 17: ("🏃", "Running sensor"), 18: ("🚴", "Cycling sensor"),
    49: ("🫁", "Pulse oximeter"), 51: ("⚖️", "Weight scale"),
}


def busctl_objects():
    out = subprocess.run(
        ["busctl", "--system", "--json=short", "call", "org.bluez", "/",
         "org.freedesktop.DBus.ObjectManager", "GetManagedObjects"],
        capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10)
    if out.returncode != 0:
        raise RuntimeError("Could not talk to Bluetooth. Is it switched on? "
                           "Try: sudo systemctl start bluetooth")
    return json.loads(out.stdout)["data"][0]


def run_scan(seconds):
    """Ask the adapter to look for devices for a few seconds."""
    with _scan_lock:
        try:
            subprocess.run(["bluetoothctl", "--timeout", str(seconds), "scan", "on"],
                           capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=seconds + 10)
        except FileNotFoundError:
            raise RuntimeError("bluetoothctl was not found. Install it: sudo dnf install bluez")
        except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
            pass


def val(props, key, default=None):
    p = props.get(key)
    return p["data"] if p else default


def describe(dev):
    icon = val(dev, "Icon")
    if icon in ICONS:
        return ICONS[icon]
    appearance = val(dev, "Appearance")
    if appearance is not None and (appearance >> 6) in APPEARANCE:
        return APPEARANCE[appearance >> 6]
    return ("✨", "Bluetooth device")


def collect(scan_seconds=0):
    if scan_seconds:
        run_scan(scan_seconds)
    objs = busctl_objects()

    adapter = None
    devices = []
    now = time.time()
    for path, ifaces in objs.items():
        if "org.bluez.Adapter1" in ifaces and adapter is None:
            a = ifaces["org.bluez.Adapter1"]
            adapter = {"name": val(a, "Alias", "Bluetooth"),
                       "powered": bool(val(a, "Powered", False)),
                       "address": val(a, "Address", "")}
        dev = ifaces.get("org.bluez.Device1")
        if not dev:
            continue

        address = val(dev, "Address", "")
        rssi = val(dev, "RSSI")
        if rssi is not None:
            _last_seen[address] = now
        name = val(dev, "Name")
        emoji, kind = describe(dev)
        makers = [COMPANIES[int(k)] for k in (val(dev, "ManufacturerData", {}) or {})
                  if int(k) in COMPANIES]
        battery = None
        if "org.bluez.Battery1" in ifaces:
            battery = val(ifaces["org.bluez.Battery1"], "Percentage")

        devices.append({
            "address": address,
            "name": name,
            "emoji": emoji,
            "kind": kind,
            "maker": makers[0] if makers else None,
            "rssi": rssi,
            "paired": bool(val(dev, "Paired", False)),
            "connected": bool(val(dev, "Connected", False)),
            "battery": battery,
            "le": val(dev, "AddressType") == "random",
            "seen_ago": round(now - _last_seen[address]) if address in _last_seen else None,
        })

    # Connected first, then strongest signal, then the rest.
    devices.sort(key=lambda d: (not d["connected"], d["rssi"] is None, -(d["rssi"] or -999)))
    return {"adapter": adapter, "devices": devices, "scan_seconds": scan_seconds}


class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def _send(self, code, body, ctype):
        data = body.encode() if isinstance(body, str) else body
        self.send_response(code)
        self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(data)))
        self.end_headers()
        self.wfile.write(data)

    def do_GET(self):
        url = urlparse(self.path)
        q = parse_qs(url.query)
        try:
            if url.path == "/":
                self._send(200, PAGE, "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            elif url.path == "/api/devices":
                scan = q.get("scan", ["0"])[0] == "1"
                data = collect(SCAN_SECONDS if scan else 0)
                self._send(200, json.dumps(data), "application/json")
            else:
                self._send(404, json.dumps({"error": "not found"}), "application/json")
        except Exception as e:
            self._send(500, json.dumps({"error": str(e)}), "application/json")

    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):
        pass


PAGE = r"""<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Bluetooth Nearby</title>
<link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml,<svg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 100 100'><text y='.9em' font-size='90'>🔵</text></svg>">
<style>
  :root {
    --sun: #ffd23f; --coral: #ff6b6b; --mint: #3ddc97; --sky: #4cc9f0;
    --grape: #9b5de5; --pink: #f15bb5; --blue: #4361ee; --ink: #2b2d42; --soft: #6c6f85;
    --card: #ffffff;
  }
  * { box-sizing: border-box; }
  body {
    margin: 0; min-height: 100vh; color: var(--ink);
    font: 15px/1.5 "Nunito", system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif;
    background: linear-gradient(135deg, #dff3ff 0%, #efe3ff 45%, #fff1d6 100%) fixed;
  }
  main { max-width: 1000px; margin: 0 auto; padding: 28px 16px 56px; }
  header { display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: space-between; gap: 16px; flex-wrap: wrap; }
  h1 { margin: 0; font-size: 30px; letter-spacing: -0.5px; }
  .logo { display: inline-grid; place-items: center; width: 44px; height: 44px; border-radius: 50%;
          background: var(--blue); color: #fff; font-size: 24px; vertical-align: middle; margin-right: 6px;
          box-shadow: 0 0 0 0 rgba(67,97,238,.5); }
  .scanning .logo { animation: pulse 1.4s ease-out infinite; }
  @keyframes pulse { 0% { box-shadow: 0 0 0 0 rgba(67,97,238,.5) } 100% { box-shadow: 0 0 0 22px rgba(67,97,238,0) } }
  .tagline { margin: 4px 0 0; color: var(--soft); }
  .btn {
    border: 0; border-radius: 999px; padding: 11px 22px; font: inherit; font-weight: 700;
    background: linear-gradient(90deg, var(--blue), var(--grape)); color: #fff; cursor: pointer;
    box-shadow: 0 6px 16px rgba(67, 97, 238, .35); transition: transform .15s;
  }
  .btn:hover { transform: translateY(-2px) scale(1.03); }
  .btn:disabled { opacity: .65; cursor: wait; transform: none; }

  .summary { display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(150px, 1fr)); gap: 12px; margin: 20px 0; }
  .pill { border-radius: 18px; padding: 14px 16px; box-shadow: 0 4px 14px rgba(0,0,0,.06); }

  .chip.on { background: var(--ink); color: #fff; }

  .legend { background: var(--card); border-radius: 18px; padding: 16px 20px; margin-top: 28px;
            box-shadow: 0 4px 14px rgba(0,0,0,.06); }
  .legend h2 { margin: 0 0 8px; font-size: 17px; }
  .legend ul { margin: 0; padding-left: 20px; }
  .legend li { margin: 4px 0; }
  .dot { display: inline-block; width: 12px; height: 12px; border-radius: 50%; vertical-align: middle; margin-right: 4px; }

  .msg.err { color: var(--coral); }
  .foot { text-align: center; color: var(--soft); font-size: 13px; margin-top: 18px; }

<body>
<main>
  <header>
    <div>
      <h1><span class="logo">ᛒ</span> Bluetooth Nearby</h1>
      <p class="tagline">Bluetooth gadgets your computer can hear around you right now.</p>
    </div>
    <button class="btn" id="scanBtn">🔍 Scan again</button>
  </header>
  <div class="progress"><span id="bar"></span></div>

  <div class="summary" id="summary"></div>
  <div class="status" id="status" hidden></div>
  <div class="filters" id="filters">
    <button class="chip on" data-f="named">✨ Named devices</button>
    <button class="chip" data-f="all">👀 Show everything</button>
    <button class="chip" data-f="mine">💜 My paired devices</button>
  </div>
  <div id="list"><div class="msg">🔍 Listening for Bluetooth devices…</div></div>

  <div class="legend">
    <h2>🌈 How to read this</h2>
    <ul>
      <li><b>Distance</b> is a rough guess from signal strength:
        <span class="dot" style="background:var(--mint)"></span>Very close (same desk)
        <span class="dot" style="background:var(--sky)"></span>Nearby (same room)
        <span class="dot" style="background:var(--sun)"></span>A bit further
        <span class="dot" style="background:var(--coral)"></span>Far away (another room or outside).
        Walls and bodies weaken the signal, so it's only a guess.</li>
      <li><b>💜 Paired</b> means your computer already knows this device. <b>✔ Connected</b> means it's in use right now.</li>
      <li><b>Unnamed devices</b> are very common. Phones, watches and earbuds often hide their name
        and change their address for privacy. The maker (like "Apple") is shown when we can tell.</li>
      <li>This page only <i>looks</i> for devices. It never pairs or connects to anything.</li>
    </ul>
  </div>
  <p class="foot">
    <label class="auto"><input type="checkbox" id="auto" checked> Scan automatically every 30 seconds</label>
    · <span id="updated"></span>
  </p>
</main>

<script>
const $ = id => document.getElementById(id);
const esc = s => String(s).replace(/[&<>"]/g, c => ({"&":"&amp;","<":"&lt;",">":"&gt;",'"':"&quot;"}[c]));
let filter = "named", lastData = null, busy = false;

function closeness(rssi) {
  if (rssi == null) return { label: "Not heard recently", emoji: "💤", color: "#c9cbd6", bg: "#f2f3f7", pct: 0 };
  const pct = Math.max(4, Math.min(100, Math.round((rssi + 100) / 60 * 100)));
  if (rssi >= -55) return { label: "Very close", emoji: "🤗", color: "var(--mint)", bg: "#dcfaea", pct };
  if (rssi >= -70) return { label: "Nearby", emoji: "😊", color: "var(--sky)", bg: "#ddf5fd", pct };
  if (rssi >= -85) return { label: "A bit further", emoji: "🙂", color: "var(--sun)", bg: "#fff6cf", pct };
  return { label: "Far away", emoji: "👋", color: "var(--coral)", bg: "#ffe1e1", pct };
}

function card(d) {
  const q = closeness(d.rssi);
  const title = d.name
    ? `<div class="name">${esc(d.name)}</div>`
    : `<div class="name unnamed">Unnamed ${d.maker ? esc(d.maker) + " device" : "device"}</div>`;
  const badges = [
    d.connected ? `<span class="badge b-conn">✔ Connected</span>` : "",
    d.paired ? `<span class="badge b-paired">💜 Paired</span>` : "",
    d.maker ? `<span class="badge b-maker">🏭 ${esc(d.maker)}</span>` : "",
    d.battery != null ? `<span class="badge b-bat">🔋 ${d.battery}%</span>` : "",
  ].join("");
  return `<div class="dev ${d.rssi == null ? "away" : ""}" style="--c:${q.color};--bg:${q.bg}">
    <div class="row">
      <div class="emoji">${d.emoji}</div>
      <div>${title}<div class="kind">${esc(d.kind)}</div></div>
    </div>
    ${badges}
    <div class="dist">
      <div class="meter"><span style="width:${q.pct}%"></span></div>
      <div class="quality"><span>${q.emoji} ${q.label}</span><small>${d.rssi != null ? d.rssi + " dBm" : ""}</small></div>
    </div>
    <div class="addr">${esc(d.address)}</div>
  </div>`;
}

function render() {
  const data = lastData;
  if (!data) return;
  const devs = data.devices;
  const inRange = devs.filter(d => d.rssi != null);
  $("summary").innerHTML = `
    <div class="pill a"><b>${inRange.length}</b>devices heard</div>
    <div class="pill b"><b>${inRange.filter(d => d.rssi >= -70).length}</b>close to you</div>
    <div class="pill c"><b>${inRange.filter(d => d.name).length}</b>with a name</div>
    <div class="pill d"><b>${devs.filter(d => d.paired).length}</b>paired with this PC</div>`;

  const a = data.adapter;
  $("status").hidden = false;
  if (!a) {
    $("status").innerHTML = "😬 No Bluetooth adapter was found on this computer.";
  } else if (!a.powered) {
    $("status").innerHTML = "😴 Bluetooth is switched <b>off</b>. Turn it on in Settings → Bluetooth, then press Scan again.";
  } else {
    $("status").innerHTML = `✅ Bluetooth is on. This computer shows up to others as <b>${esc(a.name)}</b>.`;
  }

  let shown = devs;
  if (filter === "named") shown = devs.filter(d => d.paired || (d.name && d.rssi != null));
  if (filter === "mine") shown = devs.filter(d => d.paired);
  const near = shown.filter(d => d.rssi != null || d.connected);
  const away = shown.filter(d => !(d.rssi != null || d.connected));

  let html = "";
  if (near.length) html += `<div class="grid">${near.map(card).join("")}</div>`;
  if (away.length) html += `<h2>💤 Remembered, but not heard right now</h2><div class="grid">${away.map(card).join("")}</div>`;
  if (!html) html = filter === "mine"
    ? `<div class="msg">💜 No paired devices yet.</div>`
    : `<div class="msg">🙈 Nothing to show here. Try "Show everything" or scan again.</div>`;
  $("list").innerHTML = html;
}

async function load(scan) {
  if (busy) return;
  busy = true;
  $("scanBtn").disabled = true;
  let timer;
  if (scan) {
    document.body.classList.add("scanning");
    $("scanBtn").textContent = "📡 Listening…";
    const start = Date.now();
    timer = setInterval(() => { $("bar").style.width = Math.min(100, (Date.now() - start) / 90) + "%"; }, 100);
  }
  try {
    const res = await fetch("/api/devices" + (scan ? "?scan=1" : ""));
    const data = await res.json();
    if (!res.ok) throw new Error(data.error);
    lastData = data;
    render();
    $("updated").textContent = "Last scan " + new Date().toLocaleTimeString();
  } catch (e) {
    $("list").innerHTML = `<div class="msg err">😬 ${esc(e.message)}</div>`;
  } finally {
    clearInterval(timer);
    $("bar").style.width = "0";
    document.body.classList.remove("scanning");
    busy = false;
    $("scanBtn").disabled = false;
    $("scanBtn").textContent = "🔍 Scan again";
  }
}

document.querySelectorAll(".chip").forEach(c => c.onclick = () => {
  document.querySelectorAll(".chip").forEach(x => x.classList.remove("on"));
  c.classList.add("on");
  filter = c.dataset.f;
  render();
});
$("scanBtn").onclick = () => load(true);
setInterval(() => { if ($("auto").checked) load(true); }, 30000);
load(false).then(() => load(true));
</script>
</body>
</html>
"""


def main():
    global SCAN_SECONDS
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Show nearby Bluetooth devices in a web page")
    ap.add_argument("--host", default="127.0.0.1", help="address to listen on (default: local only)")
    ap.add_argument("--port", type=int, default=8767)
    ap.add_argument("--scan-seconds", type=int, default=8, help="how long each scan listens")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    SCAN_SECONDS = max(2, args.scan_seconds)
    server = ThreadingHTTPServer((args.host, args.port), Handler)
    print(f"Bluetooth Nearby running at http://{args.host}:{args.port}  (Ctrl+C to stop)")
    try:
        server.serve_forever()
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nStopped.")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

%description
Collective Scanner is a small local web app that shows everything about your
computer in one modern dashboard with light and dark themes:
system load, memory and temperatures, disk usage, nearby Wi-Fi networks and
Bluetooth devices, your wired network and the devices on it, a port checker,
and live incoming and outgoing traffic of your own programs.
Data can be exported as CSV or PDF. It only listens on 127.0.0.1, needs no
root rights and uses only the Python standard library.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
# SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0-or-later
# Copyright (C) 2026 Denis
"""Collective Scanner - every scanner in one modern dashboard, plus live traffic.

Run:   python3 collective.py          then open http://127.0.0.1:8780
Opts:  --port 9000   --host 127.0.0.1

Sections: Overview, System, Disk, Wi-Fi, Bluetooth, Network, Ports, Traffic.
Keys 1-8 jump between sections. Light / dark theme button in the top right.

Reuses the scanners in this folder (sysmon.py, diskusage.py, wifiscan.py,
bluetoothscan.py, lanscan.py, traffic.py), so keep them next to this file.
Python standard library only - nothing to install.
"""

import argparse
import json
import webbrowser
import os
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlparse

sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import bluetoothscan  # noqa: E402
import diskusage      # noqa: E402
import lanscan        # noqa: E402
import sysmon         # noqa: E402
import traffic        # noqa: E402
import wifiscan       # noqa: E402

__version__ = "1.0.0"


def main_connection():
    conns = lanscan.connection_info()
    return conns, next((c for c in conns if c["plugged_in"] and c["ipv4"]), None)


def route(path, q):
    """Return (status, payload) for an API path."""
    arg = lambda k, d="": q.get(k, [d])[0]

    if path == "/api/sys/stats":
        with sysmon._lock:
            return 200, dict(sysmon._latest)

    if path == "/api/traffic":
        return 200, traffic.snapshot()

    if path == "/api/disk/filesystems":
        return 200, diskusage.list_filesystems()
    if path == "/api/disk/dir":
        try:
            return 200, diskusage.scan_dir(arg("path") or os.path.expanduser("~"), arg("refresh") == "1")
        except PermissionError:
            return 403, {"error": "No permission to open this folder (run with sudo to see it)"}
        except (FileNotFoundError, NotADirectoryError) as e:
            return 400, {"error": f"Not a folder: {e}"}

    if path == "/api/wifi/scan":
        return 200, {"networks": wifiscan.scan(arg("rescan") == "1"), "devices": wifiscan.wifi_status()}

    if path == "/api/bt/devices":
        return 200, bluetoothscan.collect(bluetoothscan.SCAN_SECONDS if arg("scan") == "1" else 0)

    if path == "/api/lan/connection":
        conns, main = main_connection()
        return 200, {"connections": conns, "hostname": lanscan.socket.gethostname(),
                     "health": lanscan.internet_check(main["gateway"]) if main else None}
    if path == "/api/lan/devices":
        _, main = main_connection()
        return 200, {"devices": lanscan.scan_devices(main) if main else [],
                     "network": main["ipv4"] if main else None}

    if path == "/api/ports/start":
        try:
            return 200, {"id": lanscan.start_port_job(arg("host"), arg("ports", "web"))}
        except ValueError as e:
            return 400, {"error": str(e)}
    if path == "/api/ports/status":
        try:
            return 200, lanscan.job_status(arg("id"))
        except ValueError as e:
            return 404, {"error": str(e)}
    if path == "/api/ports/cancel":
        with lanscan._jobs_lock:
            job = lanscan._jobs.get(arg("id"))
            if job:
                job["cancel"] = True
        return 200, {"ok": True}

    return 404, {"error": "not found"}


class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
    def _send(self, code, body, ctype="application/json"):
        data = body.encode() if isinstance(body, str) else body
        self.send_response(code)
        self.send_header("Content-Type", ctype)
        self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(data)))
        self.send_header("Cache-Control", "no-store")
        self.end_headers()
        self.wfile.write(data)

    def do_GET(self):
        url = urlparse(self.path)
        if url.path == "/":
            self._send(200, PAGE, "text/html; charset=utf-8")
            return
        try:
            code, payload = route(url.path, parse_qs(url.query))
        except Exception as e:  # keep the server alive, show the error in the page
            code, payload = 500, {"error": str(e)}
        self._send(code, json.dumps(payload))

    def log_message(self, fmt, *args):
        pass


PAGE = r"""<!doctype html>
<html lang="en">
<head>
<meta charset="utf-8">
<meta name="viewport" content="width=device-width, initial-scale=1">
<title>Collective Scanner</title>
<link rel="icon" href="data:image/svg+xml,<svg xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg' viewBox='0 0 32 32'><defs><linearGradient id='g' x1='0' y1='0' x2='1' y2='1'><stop offset='0' stop-color='%232f6bff'/><stop offset='1' stop-color='%2314c8b0'/></linearGradient></defs><rect width='32' height='32' rx='9' fill='url(%23g)'/><circle cx='16' cy='16' r='8' fill='none' stroke='white' stroke-width='2.5'/><circle cx='16' cy='16' r='2.6' fill='white'/></svg>">
<script>
  /* pick the theme before anything is drawn, so the page never flashes */
  (function () {
    let t = null; try { t = localStorage.getItem("cs-theme"); } catch (e) {}
    if (t !== "light" && t !== "dark") t = matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)").matches ? "dark" : "light";
    document.documentElement.dataset.theme = t;
  })();
</script>
<style>
  :root, :root[data-theme="light"] {
    /* soft pastel light theme - muted, low-glare */
    --bg: #ccd2de; --surface: #d9dee8; --surface2: #d1d7e2; --border: #bcc4d3; --track: #c4cbd8;
    --text: #2a3145; --muted: #515a70; --faint: #78819a;
    --accent: #6681bb; --accent-soft: #c4cee4; --teal: #5b9f96; --teal-soft: #c3d8d5;
    --violet: #8d7dbb; --good: #5f9a7e; --good-soft: #c6dacf; --warn: #b8955a; --warn-soft: #dcd1bd;
    --bad: #b77a89; --bad-soft: #dcc8ce;
    --shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(40, 48, 68, .05), 0 2px 10px rgba(40, 48, 68, .05);
    --side: #cfd5e1;
  }
  :root[data-theme="dark"] {
    --bg: #0b1222; --surface: #121b2f; --surface2: #172239; --border: #22304d; --track: #1d2a44;
    --text: #e8eefb; --muted: #93a1bd; --faint: #5f6e8c;
    --accent: #5b8cff; --accent-soft: #1b2b52; --teal: #2dd4bf; --teal-soft: #113a3d;
    --violet: #a78bfa; --good: #34d399; --good-soft: #10382c; --warn: #fbbf24; --warn-soft: #3a2f12;
    --bad: #fb7185; --bad-soft: #3d1a25;
    --shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(0, 0, 0, .3), 0 8px 28px rgba(0, 0, 0, .25);
    --side: #0f182b;
  }
  * { box-sizing: border-box; }
  html { background: var(--bg); }
  body { margin: 0; background: var(--bg); color: var(--text); font: 14px/1.5 Inter, "Adwaita Sans", Cantarell, system-ui, -apple-system, "Segoe UI", sans-serif;
         font-feature-settings: "tnum" 1, "cv11" 1; -webkit-font-smoothing: antialiased; transition: background .25s, color .25s; }
  .mono { font-family: "JetBrains Mono", "Adwaita Mono", "Source Code Pro", ui-monospace, monospace; font-size: 12.5px; }
  button, input { font: inherit; color: inherit; }
  a { color: var(--accent); cursor: pointer; text-decoration: none; }
  a:hover { text-decoration: underline; }

  /* ---------- layout ---------- */
  .app { display: grid; grid-template-columns: 248px 1fr; min-height: 100vh; }
  .side { background: var(--side); border-right: 1px solid var(--border); padding: 22px 14px; position: sticky; top: 0; height: 100vh;
          display: flex; flex-direction: column; gap: 22px; }
  .brand { display: flex; align-items: center; gap: 11px; padding: 0 8px; }
  .logo { width: 38px; height: 38px; border-radius: 11px; background: linear-gradient(135deg, var(--accent), var(--teal)); display: grid; place-items: center;
          box-shadow: 0 6px 16px color-mix(in srgb, var(--accent) 35%, transparent); flex: none; }
  .brand b { display: block; font-size: 15.5px; font-weight: 800; letter-spacing: -.2px; }
